Forwarding it exposed a second issue: terminal ownership used the registry's global preferred run-ID match, then discarded a mismatched child session without searching the correct scope.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThe Gateway now forwards its existing prepared `runId`. Terminal ownership resolves the task through the canonical task-registry scope `{ runId, childSessionKey }`, then records the exact private task ID used by lifecycle cleanup.\\n\\nThis is the complete owner-boundary repair. It adds no config, protocol, SDK, migration, persistence, identity, fallback, or cache surface. The detached-task runtime retains its existing global lookup because it has a different ownership contract.\\n\\nThis change was developed with AI assistance and rewritten by a maintainer after ClawSweeper found the cross-child collision.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nCompleting one Gateway/loopback task now closes that task's PTY even when another child session reuses the same run ID. The sibling task's terminal remains live and writable.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- **Red/green collision regression:** before the rewrite, the terminal lookup received only `shared-run`; the new duplicate-run-ID test failed because the child session was absent. After the rewrite, both terminal and Gateway regressions pass and assert lookup by `(\\\"shared-run\\\", childSessionKey)`.\\n- **Focused tests:** 86 assertions passed across `terminal-tool`, Gateway resolver/exclusion, terminal session lifecycle, runtime subscriptions, task status access, and the two registry cross-scope collision cases.\\n- **Changed gate:** Blacksmith Testbox `tbx_01kzwqhv8vfftahjzk8tj8thq4`, run https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/actions/runs/31668517679, completed with exit 0. Task 1 was the registry's global preferred match, so the pre-rewrite lookup would leave task 2's terminal unbound. On the rewritten head, terminalizing task 2 reduced live PTYs from two to one; task 1 stayed writable and emitted output after cleanup.\\n\\nHarness SHA-256: Gateway.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n### Before\\n\\nA limited browser incorrectly displayed the Gateway-update error:\\n\\n![Custodian showing the false Gateway update \"received_events_url\": \"https://api.github.com/users/steipete/received_events\", \"type\": \"User\", \"user_view_type\": \"public\", \"site_admin\": false }, \"body\": \"## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nThe durable node worker supervisor can report that a turn completed or failed, but its private Gateway receipt exposes only identity and state. A Gateway-side node launch client therefore cannot collect the bounded worker runtime JSON that the existing turn launcher consumes, or the redacted diagnostic for a failed child.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThis moves the private launch/request/identity DTOs and plan hash into a worker-owned shared contract, then makes terminal receipts a closed discriminated union:\\n\\n- `pending` / `running` carry identity and state only;\\n- `completed` additionally carries bounded, valid JSON (`resultJson`);\\n- `failed` / `interrupted` / `cancelled` additionally carry bounded single-line `errorText`.\\n\\nThe node-host projection validates every durable receipt before it crosses the private capability-bound transport. Inconsistent rows fail closed as a generic supervisor error. PIDs, process identities, gateway namespace, descriptor, credential, timestamps, and filesystem internals remain absent. Request parsing and plan hashing keep one canonical worker-owned implementation; the obsolete node-host identity module is removed.\\n\\nThis is deliberately a prerequisite, not device turn launch. It does not enable `sessions.dispatch({ deviceId })`, create a node tunnel, install bundles, synthesize `SpawnResult`, or bypass the existing workspace-transfer gate.\\n\\nThe patch also repairs an inherited red-main CI failure by renaming an internal migration-assertion map that accidentally matched the `OPENCLAW_*` environment-variable ratchet. The ratchet stays at 502; no environment surface is added.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nNo user-visible device-runner capability is enabled yet. This gives the next Gateway adapter a strict, replayable terminal-result seam without exposing node-host internals or weakening the private supervisor boundary.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Pre-fix regression: `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs src/node-host/invoke-worker-supervisor.test.ts` failed because a completed receipt omitted `resultJson`.\\n- Broad supervisor proof: 61 tests passed across the shared protocol, worker runtime result, private invocation, real process supervision/recovery, and node runtime integration.\\n- Shared wire-contract proof: 15 closed-shape/bounds tests passed, including malformed/extra fields, missing terminal data, invalid JSON, multiline diagnostics, and byte ceilings.\\n- Red-main repair proof: `OPENCLAW_* count 502/502`; `src/state/openclaw-state-db.test.ts` passed 142 tests with one skip.\\n- Canonical changed gate passed after trusted-source local fallback because no remote provider was ready: formatting, unchanged Plugin SDK API baseline, dead exports, production/test types, full core lint, schema/database/auth/pairing guards, and zero runtime cycles.\\n- Final structured review and secret scan: clean.\\n- `git diff --check`: clean.\\n\\nLOC classification against the branch base:\\n\\n- production: +322/−181, net +141;\\n- tests: +138/−7, net +131.\\n\\nThe production growth is the strict terminal receipt parser and bounded union required by the forthcoming launch adapter; moving the existing request/identity/hash contract removes duplicate ownership rather than preserving a compatibility facade.\\n\", \"user_view_type\": \"public\", \"site_admin\": false }, \"body\": \"## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nFixes an issue where the Parallels npm update smoke could leave a post-update gateway unavailable when its first launch intentionally exited after detecting changed plugin migration inputs. The harness ignored that owned refusal and either performed an unrelated blind relaunch or waited to the readiness timeout.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThe macOS, Linux, and Windows update scripts now track the exact launched process and its launch-specific log. They restart once only when that process exited without an observed POSIX signal and its current-launch log contains the exact startup migration refusal prefix. A live process, stale refusal, generic exit, signaled exit, or second refusal fails without another restart, while retaining the original total readiness deadline.\\n\\nThis changes release-validation tooling only. It does not alter candidate packages, tags, npm state, or runtime behavior.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nRelease operators can complete the documented one-restart migration convergence path in the Parallels update matrix without masking unrelated startup failures.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs test/scripts/parallels-npm-update-smoke.test.ts` (42 passed)\\n- generated macOS update script: `bash -n` passed\\n- generated Linux update script: `bash -n` passed\\n- targeted `oxfmt` check passed\\n- targeted `oxlint` passed\\n- `git diff --check` passed\\n\\nProduction vs test delta: `+101/-25` script lines, `+36/-7` focused test lines. The production growth adds the required cross-platform process ownership, current-launch log boundary, exact refusal gate, and one-restart invariant.\\n\\n## Review Follow-up\\n\\nClawSweeper reported no concrete finding. Its optional rank-up move asks for an after-fix Parallels refusal/recovery trace. The release owner is accepting the exact-head source, focused test, generated-script syntax, changed-gate, and CI proof for landing because the real exact-byte rerun requires this harness commit to Solves\\n\\nResolves a problem where the shared `checks-ui` worker could fail unrelated Control UI changes when a predecessor suite cached the real clipboard module, a lazy update dialog retained module state across jsdom suites, or a delayed tooltip callback ran after Vitest replaced the browser realm.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThe update-card suite now uses the shared in-app confirmation helpers, releases modal-owner state before removing DOM hosts, resets lazy modules, and runs in the existing singleton-sensitive UI project. The task-suggestion regression drives the actual Clipboard API and fallback owners instead of asserting a later mock export that production may not reference. Tooltip trigger detection now uses the HTML namespace rather than the transient global `HTMLElement`, which is also correct for HTML nodes created by another document realm.\\n\\nThis is the owner-boundary fix: it preserves the production clipboard error path and update-confirmation behavior, while removing test-order coupling instead of adding waits or weakening assertions.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nNo user-facing workflow changes. Maintainers get deterministic Control UI CI, and tooltip triggers remain correct across document realms.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\nPre-fix reproductions:\\n\\n- `chat-pane-terminal.test.ts` followed by `chat-pane-task-suggestions.test.ts` in one `isolate:false` worker failed because the asserted clipboard mock had zero calls while the production pane retained the real import.\\n- Exact local `checks-ui` packing emitted `ReferenceError: HTMLElement is not defined` from a delayed tooltip slot callback after jsdom teardown.\\n- The new cross-document tooltip regression failed before the owner fix with `expected null to be truthy`.\\n\\nPost-fix proof:\\n\\n- Sidebar update-card file alone: 24/24, three consecutive runs.\\n- Task-suggestion file alone: 5/5, three consecutive runs.\\n- Both files together: 29/29, three consecutive runs.\\n- Owner neighborhood: 59/59 across tooltip, update runtime, sidebar, task suggestions, and the known clipboard predecessor.\\n- Exact full UI command: 623 files passed; 9,073 tests passed, one skipped; no post-teardown `HTMLElement` exception.\\n- `tsgo:ui`, `tsgo:core:test`, core/UI oxlint, scripts lint, UI stylelint, and UI i18n verification passed.\\n- Autoreview: clean, no accepted/actionable findings.\\n\\nProduction LOC: +6/-4 (net +2) | Tests/test support: +65/-20 (net +45)\\n\\nProvenance: lazy update confirmation and its original sidebar test flow came from #121234 (commit `95fdce70493`, code/PR author and merger @vyctorbrzezowski, 2026-08-09) and was extended by #121686 (commit `e7ba2e1f6b1`, same author/merger, 2026-08-11). The fragile clipboard export mock came from #121259 (commit `5207c4765d7`, code/PR author and merger @steipete, 2026-08-09). The global-realm tooltip check was carried forward from #100024 (commit `65e12328aa20`, code author Shakker, PR author/merger @shakkernerd, 2026-07-04).\\n\\nBest-fix verdict: best. Isolating every affected chat test would preserve the wrong clipboard assertion and slow the shared lane; arbitrary render waits would not repair module identity or teardown ownership; adding test-only production injection would create a seam no product caller needs.\\n\\nOne broader `check:changed` attempt stopped on unrelated current-main max-lines ratchet entries for removed OpenAI realtime-voice files. pruned stale entries or enforced the entry cap.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nPinned sessions now use the same shared maintenance-immunity predicate as archived sessions. The change covers both age pruning and count capping, including the SQLite write-triggered path that owns the persisted row and transcript.\\n\\nThis PR intentionally does not change how protected entries are counted against `maxEntries`; that separate quota-accounting defect is handled by #123014.\\n\\nAI-assisted; the implementation and tests were reviewed against the full maintenance path.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nPinned conversations and their transcripts remain available until the user explicitly unpins or deletes them, even when session maintenance runs.\\n\\n## Regression Window\\n\\nPinning was introduced by [#98510](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/98510) on July 4, 2026 without adding `pinnedAt` to the maintenance-preservation predicate. Pnpm could not resolve the package's private `workspace:*` devDependency and stopped before producing the OpenClaw candidate tarball.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThe Docker package owner now reuses the source ref's existing package-manifest lifecycle around only the inner `packages/ai` pack. It restores the AI manifest bytes before tarball discovery and outer OpenClaw packaging, forwards the resolved lifecycle from the outer packager, preserves the original pack error when restoration succeeds, and retains both errors when pack and restoration fail. Frozen refs without the lifecycle keep the existing no-op behavior.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nInstaller and release validation can package source candidates containing the split `@openclaw/ai` workspace without requiring a candidate-local install solely to resolve private development dependencies.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Pre-fix regression: the focused package-Docker suite failed because the AI workspace devDependency remained present during pack and because the outer packager did not forward the manifest lifecycle.\\n- Focused suite: `32 passed`, `3 skipped`.\\n- Changed gates: conflict, attribution, registry, boundary, dependency, formatting, patch, temp-dir, script typecheck, and test-root typecheck lanes passed on Blacksmith Testbox run `31661700617`; the surfaced script lint finding passed its classified narrow retry.\\n- Installer smoke: Blacksmith Testbox run `31661901465` built the candidate and crossed the original `packages/ai` pack failure. It then exposed a separate later failure where outer `npm pack --json` exceeded the existing 1 MiB captured-stdout limit; that is intentionally outside this two-file lifecycle repair.\\n- Local ClawSweeper reviewed the committed diff at high confidence. Its one low finding noted that an abrupt `SIGKILL` could strand the nested AI manifest receipt because root postpack recovery owns only root artifacts. That finding is not applied here because a correct fix requires either the third-file recovery owner or a staged-copy redesign, outside this explicitly authorized two-file repair and LOC caps; normal signals, caught pack failures, and successful paths restore exact bytes.\\n- Production delta: `+25` lines net. \"user_view_type\": \"public\", \"site_admin\": false }, \"body\": \"## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nFixes an issue where creating a Discord thread with an initial message longer than 2,000 characters could fail at Discord's message boundary. Forum and media starter messages were rejected before thread creation, while ordinary threads could be created first and then fail to receive their initial message, leaving an empty thread and a partial result.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\n`createThreadDiscord` owns the thread-create transaction, but it previously bypassed the existing Discord text chunking and message-create retry contracts. This change reuses those canonical helpers at that boundary: forum/media threads put the first Discord-valid chunk in the required single starter message and send any remainder afterward, while ordinary threads create first and then send all initial-content chunks. Follow-ups retain stable nonce retries, confirmed delivery progress, and the distinction between a definitely undelivered chunk and an ambiguous delivery outcome.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nUsers can create Discord forum, media, and ordinary threads with initial content longer than 2,000 characters and have the complete text delivered across Discord-valid messages. Sub-limit multiline content remains one message. If a later chunk fails for an unrelated reason, the partial result preserves the confirmed delivered prefix and does not falsely report an ambiguous send as absent. No configuration changes are required.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\nA read-only upstream assessment confirmed that the equivalent fix was not already present on remote `main` before the branch was refreshed. The refresh had one real import-block conflict with an independent Discord search/retry-after change; the resolution retained both changes, and the effective fix remained uncovered by `main`.\\n\\nThe fixed candidate ran the real message CLI, a live Gateway, the bundled Discord action runtime, account/proxy resolution, the Discord REST client, and HTTP CONNECT/TLS transport. Only the unavailable external Discord account/API was replaced by a local HTTPS contract server enforcing Discord's 2,000-character content limit.\\n\\n```text\\nGATEWAY_POST_READY_SETTLED agent_runtime_plugins_pre_warmed=true\\nWARMUP text thread-create through CLI -> live Gateway -> Discord action runtime\\nWARMUP_COMPLETE cli_exit=0 action_successes=1 delivered=true\\nTRIGGER forum CLI -> live Gateway -> Discord action runtime\\nTEXT_CLI_COMPLETE exit=0 requests=7 stderr_length=0\\nGATEWAY message.action success_count=2 failure_count=0\\nTRANSPORT CONNECT discord.com:443 count=6 tls_errors=0 unrelated_connects=0\\nTRANSITION forum starter_and_followups=[2000,1] max_payload=2000\\nOUTCOME forum thread_id=100000000000000101 reconstructed_length=2001 content_match=true rejected_payloads=0 cli_partial=false\\nTRANSITION text followups=[2000,1] max_payload=2000\\nOUTCOME text thread_id=100000000000000102 reconstructed_length=2001 content_match=true rejected_payloads=0 cli_partial=false\\nLIVE_E2E_PASS gateway_ready=true forum_complete=true text_complete=true\\n```\\n\\nRegression checks on the exact candidate head:\\n\\n- Both affected Vitest files: 196 tests What Problem This Solves\\n\\nFixes an issue where users uninstalling the gateway service would be told it was not loaded while an unloaded service definition remained installed. On macOS, the leftover LaunchAgent plist could be loaded again at a later login even though `openclaw uninstall --service` had completed.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThe command now uses the load probe only to decide whether a separate stop step is needed. It always delegates removal to the platform's idempotent uninstall implementation, including when the service is not loaded or the load probe fails.\\n\\nThis deliberately does not add system-level LaunchDaemon management; that broader product decision remains in #14251.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nOperators can now remove disabled, unloaded, or otherwise unprobeable gateway service definitions with the normal uninstall command. The implementation and tests were produced with Codex and manually reviewed against the command entry point, all three platform uninstall implementations, sibling reset/configure flows, current `main`, and relevant file history. \"public\", \"site_admin\": false }, \"body\": \"## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nAfter #122941, the same background task could show two different detail views at once: clicking an inline subagent activity row opened the new Details sidebar panel, while clicking that task inside the background-tasks rail opened a separate compact detail *inside the rail* (prompt/output, no transcript, ~280px wide). Two entry points, two divergent detail UIs over one task ledger — spotted by the maintainer as \\\"I see it twice; that is very illogical.\\\"\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nOne canonical task-detail surface. Every task click — inline subagent activity row or rail row — now routes through the same `handleOpenSidebar({ kind: \\\"task\\\", taskId })` path into the Details sidebar panel, and the rail becomes a pure list with an `aria-current` highlight on the task whose panel is open. The panel generalizes from subagent-only to every runtime: it gains a runtime label in the status line, keeps the live event-driven `chat.history` transcript (throttled refetch on task events, final read at terminal transition), and falls back to the prompt/output inspector blocks when the task has no transcript session. One deliberate rule, commented in code: a task whose transcript session resolves to the pane's own canonical session shows the inspector instead — mirroring the current conversation into its own sidebar is exactly the duplication this PR removes.\\n\\nThe rail's private view machine is deleted outright: the list → compact detail → one-shot transcript states, back button, focus choreography, in-rail transcript rendering, the `backgroundTaskTranscript` controller in `ChatProps`/`ChatPaneBase`, the subagent transcript gate, dead i18n keys, and ~180 lines of rail-detail CSS. `onSelectTask` was collapsed into `onOpenTaskDetail` (two names for one action). The rail keeps everything list-shaped: running/finished sections, stop buttons, refresh, badge, collapse, and narrow-layout bottom dock.\\n\\nLive testing and review caught three regressions, each fixed with a failing-first regression test: (1) closing the panel via the region's Close Details collapses the layout but retains `sidebarContent`, so the rail row stayed highlighted for an invisible panel — the highlight now gates on detail-slot visibility via a shared `detailSlotOpen` helper; (2) a queued subagent with no child session yet fell back to its requester `sessionKey`, rendering the parent conversation as the task transcript — subagents now use only `childSessionKey`, otherwise the inspector; (3) a failed `tasks.get` permanently suppressed the panel's render-driven detail load — selection now clears the recorded error so reopening retries (render alone still must not, or an error would retry every paint).\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nClicking a task anywhere opens one consistent, resizable detail panel (260–1200px) with live status, Stop, and transcript where one exists; the rail shows which task is open and never duplicates the view. Net production delta is strongly negative (~−430 production LOC; ~−690 overall with tests).\\n\\nRelease-note context: Control UI background-task clicks (rail rows and inline subagent activity) now open one shared task-detail sidebar with live status and transcript; the rail's in-place detail and transcript views are gone.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\nRail → sidebar flow (open rail, click task, pushed completion updates panel, list stays intact):\\n\\nhttps://github.com/user-attachments/assets/95cdc6a9-fc01-4876-acea-09bc154c52a0\\n\\nInline subagent activity → same panel:\\n\\nhttps://github.com/user-attachments/assets/772f3de4-af82-440c-ab9c-dea4c916bb8a\\n\\nRail list (pure list, no in-rail detail) and the shared panel with runtime label + transcript:\\n\\n![Rail open](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/68c1a781-214e-43bc-ab13-c83622c8e2f2)\\n\\n![Task detail sidebar](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/d30f79d1-252a-43cc-ba71-3effa33de981)\\n\\n- Browser E2E (`background-tasks.e2e.test.ts`, videos above are its recordings): rail click opens `[data-task-detail-panel]` with the child transcript and asserts the `chat.history` session key; pushed completion updates the open panel and regroups the list; cancel works from the list without opening the panel; the open row carries `aria-current`/highlight and **loses it when the panel closes** (this assertion fails without the highlight fix); the main conversation URL and transcript stay untouched throughout.\\n- Unit: 319 focused tests across the rail, activity rows, panel, and loader state pass; view-machine-only tests were deleted with the views they proved.\\n- `pnpm tsgo:ui`, `pnpm ui:i18n:baseline`/`verify` (no drift), `pnpm lint:ui:styles`, scoped `check-changed` Testbox gate — all pass.\\n- Live-verified against `pnpm dev:ui:mock`: rail open → row click → panel (runtime label, elapsed, Stop), finished task → inspector fallback, inline row click highlights the rail row, region close clears panel + highlight, zero console errors.\\n\\nAI-assisted implementation and review.\\n\", \"created_at\": \"2026-08-13T04:39:59Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-08-13T04:51:21Z\", \"closed_at\": \"2026-08-13T04:51:07Z\", \"merged_at\": \"2026-08-13T04:51:07Z\", message. This was observed in PR #122955 CI run 31666366638 even though the timeout code remained correct.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThe headless path capped both the guest timeout and the host worker watchdog to the same remaining wall-clock deadline. The two timers could therefore expire together, letting the internal watchdog win the message race. The worker watchdog now retains the existing two-second grace used by the resumable Code Mode paths, while the headless abort scope remains the sole owner of the wall-clock deadline.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nHeadless wall-clock expiry is now classified and reported consistently. Guest execution time and the outer wall-clock limit are unchanged; only the later host watchdog keeps its intended cleanup grace.\\n\\nAI-assisted; I reviewed and understand the change.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Before: [PR #122955 CI run 31666366638](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/actions/runs/31666366638), `agentic-agents-core-runtime`, failed with expected `code mode timeout exceeded` and received `code mode worker timeout exceeded`.\\n- Before, standalone Linux reproduction: [Blacksmith Testbox run 31666974106](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/actions/runs/31666974106) failed `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs src/agents/code-mode-headless.test.ts` with the same message race, ruling out sibling fake-timer leakage.\\n- After, rebased head: [Blacksmith Testbox run 31667289740](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/actions/runs/31667289740) passed the same focused file, 64/64 tests.\\n- `node_modules/.bin/oxfmt --check src/agents/code-mode-headless.ts` passed on Testbox.\\n- `git diff --check` passed.\\n- Structured Codex autoreview reported no accepted/actionable findings.\\n- `node scripts/check-changed.mjs -- src/agents/code-mode-headless.ts` reached the repository env-var ratchet but is currently blocked by unrelated base drift: current `origin/main` contains 503 distinct `OPENCLAW_*` names against the existing budget of 502. This patch adds no environment variable.\\n\\nProduction LOC: +3/-4 (net -1). \"user_view_type\": \"public\", \"site_admin\": false }, \"body\": \"Related: #111745\\r\\nCloses #121059\\r\\n\\r\\n> **AI-assisted.** Investigated and patched with Claude. I reproduced the\\r\\n> behaviour myself before and after the change, and can explain each edit.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What Problem This Solves\\r\\n\\r\\n`normalizeNpmVersionDrift()` in `scripts/generate-npm-package-lock.mts` strips\\r\\n`libc` from every entry of the generated `package-lock.json`. The comment says\\r\\nwhy:\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\n// npm versions and mutable registry metadata disagree on these package-lock\\r\\n// fields. None affect resolution, so keep generated npm locks stable.\\r\\ndelete metadata.deprecated;\\r\\ndelete metadata.libc;\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThat holds for `deprecated` and `peer`. It does not hold for `libc`. npm reads\\r\\n`libc` alongside `os`/`cpu` to choose between glibc and musl builds, and for\\r\\npackages that ship both, the two variants are identical in `os` and `cpu`. `libc`\\r\\nis the only thing telling them apart. Delete it and npm has no basis to choose,\\r\\nso it installs both.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is live in our own dependency tree. From `pnpm-lock.yaml`:\\r\\n\\r\\n```yaml\\r\\n'@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-linux-arm64-musl@0.3.219':\\r\\n cpu: [arm64]\\r\\n os: [linux]\\r\\n libc: [musl]\\r\\n\\r\\n'@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-linux-arm64@0.3.219':\\r\\n cpu: [arm64]\\r\\n os: [linux]\\r\\n libc: [glibc]\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n84 packages in `pnpm-lock.yaml` carry a `libc` constraint. Any consumer\\r\\ninstalling from a generated lock gets both variants of all of them.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\r\\n\\r\\nThe original intent was keeping generated locks byte-stable across npm versions,\\r\\nwhich is a real concern, not a hypothetical one. npm 10 emits no `libc` at all.\\r\\nnpm 11 emits it. `createNpmLockCommand()` resolves whatever npm sits beside the\\r\\nrunning node, and our supported Node floors straddle the change: Node 22.22.3\\r\\nships npm 10.9.x, Node 24.15 ships npm 11.x.\\r\\n\\r\\nSo deleting the line and stopping there would fix correctness and reintroduce the\\r\\nchurn. This change backfills `libc` from `pnpm-lock.yaml` instead. That file is\\r\\nalready the source of truth for versions and integrities here, via\\r\\n`assertNpmLockMatchesPnpmLock` and `readPnpmLockPackageIntegrities`, and the new\\r\\n`readPnpmLockPackageLibc()` mirrors the latter.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe output ends up both correct and stable, and stable for a better reason than\\r\\nbefore: the value comes from a file in the repo rather than from whichever npm\\r\\nhappened to run. `deprecated` and `peer` are still dropped, since those really\\r\\ndon't affect resolution. Entries the pnpm lock doesn't constrain still have\\r\\n`libc` removed, so npm 11's output keeps converging with npm 10's. The field is\\r\\ndeleted before being reassigned, so a backfilled `libc` occupies the same key\\r\\nposition as one npm 11 emitted, keeping output byte-identical either way.\\r\\n\\r\\nWorth noting the two npm versions only disagree about *writing* the field. Both\\r\\nread it: npm 10.9.7 running `npm ci` against a lockfile containing `libc`\\r\\ninstalled only the glibc variant. So pinning npm to 11 would also fix this, but\\r\\nit would force the toolchain on every contributor and break the Node 22 floor.\\r\\nReading from a committed file constrains nobody.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis was originally written against `scripts/generate-npm-package-lock.mjs` and\\r\\n`.d.mts`. It is now retargeted to `scripts/generate-npm-package-lock.mts` after\\r\\nthe generator was migrated to TypeScript upstream — the `.mjs` is a tsx shim now,\\r\\nthe `.d.mts` is gone, and the `delete metadata.libc` had carried over unchanged\\r\\ninto the new file.\\r\\n\\r\\n## User Impact\\r\\n\\r\\nAnyone installing from a generated lock stops receiving the wrong-libc copy of\\r\\nevery libc-discriminated package. On a glibc host each one drops from two\\r\\ninstalled copies to one.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe wasted copies are native binaries. For `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` the\\r\\nduplicate measures 258 MB.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Evidence\\r\\n\\r\\n### End-to-end proof: patched generator to installed tree\\r\\n\\r\\nMeasured at exact head `6133f6038a`, against base `f806abf43b`, on Node 24.19.0 /\\r\\nnpm 11.17.0, Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), glibc 2.39.\\r\\n\\r\\n`extensions/acpx` depends transitively on `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`, whose\\r\\nLinux builds are the glibc/musl pair. Generating that package's lock with each\\r\\nversion of the generator, then installing from it:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n# main (current behaviour)\\r\\nlock for extensions/acpx: 185 entries\\r\\nentries carrying libc: 0\\r\\n\\r\\n$ npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund\\r\\nadded 147 packages\\r\\n$ ls node_modules/@anthropic-ai/\\r\\nclaude-agent-sdk claude-agent-sdk-linux-x64 claude-agent-sdk-linux-x64-musl sdk\\r\\n$ du -sh node_modules\\r\\n889M\\r\\n\\r\\n# patched\\r\\nlock for extensions/acpx: 185 entries\\r\\nentries carrying libc: 4\\r\\n @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-linux-arm64 libc=glibc\\r\\n @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-linux-arm64-musl libc=musl\\r\\n @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-linux-x64 libc=glibc\\r\\n @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-linux-x64-musl libc=musl\\r\\n\\r\\n$ npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund\\r\\nadded 146 packages\\r\\n$ ls node_modules/@anthropic-ai/\\r\\nclaude-agent-sdk claude-agent-sdk-linux-x64 sdk\\r\\n$ du -sh node_modules\\r\\n632M\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nSame 185 lock entries either way. On a glibc host main's lock installs the musl\\r\\nbinary as well; the patched lock does not. That duplicate is 258 MB of native\\r\\nbinary that cannot execute on the host:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n$ du -sh node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-linux-x64-musl\\r\\n258M\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReproduced by calling the generator's exported `generateNpmPackageLock` and\\r\\nwriting the result alongside `packageJsonForNpmLock(...)`, then `npm ci` from it.\\r\\nSwitch between the two behaviours with\\r\\n`git checkout f806abf43b -- scripts/generate-npm-package-lock.mts`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### npm's libc behaviour, measured directly\\r\\n\\r\\n`rollup@4.53.5`'s linux-x64 variants differ only in `libc`:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nrollup-linux-x64-gnu os: ['linux'] cpu: ['x64'] libc: ['glibc']\\r\\nrollup-linux-x64-musl os: ['linux'] cpu: ['x64'] libc: ['musl']\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n`npm ci` from the same lockfile, before and after stripping `libc`:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nA: libc intact -> rollup-linux-x64-gnu (1)\\r\\nB: libc deleted -> rollup-linux-x64-gnu, rollup-linux-x64-musl (2)\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe version disagreement that motivated the original delete, same `package.json`\\r\\nboth times, each run in a clean directory:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nnpm 10.9.7 -> libc emitted on 0 entries\\r\\nnpm 11.17.0 -> libc emitted on 11 entries\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nCorroborated upstream by npm/cli#8514: npm emits `libc` in package-lock.json only\\r\\nfrom npm 11 onward, and inconsistently.\\r\\n\\r\\nAnd the asymmetry, npm 10.9.7 running `npm ci` against a lockfile that has `libc`:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nrollup-linux-x64-gnu (1)\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### Local validation\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\npnpm check passed — typecheck core / ui / generator\\r\\npnpm vitest run test/scripts/generate-npm-package-lock.test.ts -> 28 passed\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe package-lock guard is the useful one: it runs\\r\\n`node scripts/generate-npm-package-lock.mjs --all` and revalidated every lock in\\r\\nthe repo against the patched generator, with no drift. `pnpm test` in full was\\r\\nnot run locally; relying on CI for that.\\r\\n\\r\\nI updated the existing `normalizes npm patch-version metadata drift` case to\\r\\nassert `libc` survives, and added two cases: backfill when npm omits the field,\\r\\nand removal when the pnpm lock does not constrain it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### autoreview\\r\\n\\r\\nBranch mode, base `f806abf43b`, engine claude:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\ntrufflehog: clean\\r\\nautoreview clean: no An earlier run flagged a key-ordering asymmetry between backfilled and\\r\\nnpm-11-emitted `libc`; that is addressed by deleting before reassigning, and the\\r\\ncurrent run confirms output is byte-identical regardless of which npm generated\\r\\nthe input.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes\\r\\n\\r\\nI found this while investigating #111745, so to be clear: this is not a fix for\\r\\nthat issue. #111745 concerns `os`/`cpu` filtering on `@openai/codex`; this is the\\r\\nsame bug class on a different field, found separately. \"user_view_type\": \"public\", \"site_admin\": false }, \"body\": \"## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nFixes an issue where `memory_search` could spend the entire request budget reconciling a large session transcript corpus before querying an already usable builtin index.\\n\\nFixes #120671\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\n`MemoryIndexManager.search` now backgrounds only session-only reconciliation (`sessionsDirty && !dirty`) while continuing to await ordinary dirty-memory synchronization. This keeps an existing indexed search responsive during session catch-up without allowing ordinary memory-file edits to be queried before their index update completes.\\n\\nThe existing synchronous empty-index bootstrap, sync admission/close ownership, and query cancellation behavior are unchanged.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nSearches over an existing valid index can return while session reconciliation continues, so session results may be temporarily stale. Ordinary indexed-memory edits remain fresh because search still waits for their synchronization.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Before-fix baseline proof was captured on `0a6a1d94192b5ffe3532df0195ac383f53b4b772`; exact rebased head proof was rerun on `25bf4eb2cc2d7ec0de1e1f8b4b7ba62402c9ab30`.\\n- Base: session-only search did not return before the delayed sync gate (`elapsedBeforeReleaseMs=701`, `returnedBeforeSyncRelease=false`); ordinary dirty search also waited.\\n- Rebased head: session-only search returned one real indexed result in `4ms` before the sync gate released (`returnedBeforeSyncRelease=true`); ordinary dirty search waited `701ms` and returned one result. The unchanged negative control returned zero results.\\n- Focused regression: `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs extensions/memory-core/src/memory/manager-async-state.test.ts extensions/memory-core/src/memory/manager-search-orchestration.test.ts` — 2 files passed, including session-only background sync, background error reporting, and ordinary dirty sync waiting.\\n\\nReproduction from the repository root (copy the source below into `builtin-real-proof.mjs` first):\\n\\n```text\\n$ OPENCLAW_BUILTIN_PROOF_EXPECT_FIX=0 node --import tsx builtin-real-proof.mjs\\nbase 0a6a1d94192: session returnedBeforeSyncRelease=false; ordinary returnedBeforeSyncRelease=false; negativeControl=0\\n$ node --import tsx builtin-real-proof.mjs\\nhead 25bf4eb2cc2: session returnedBeforeSyncRelease=true; ordinary returnedBeforeSyncRelease=false; negativeControl=0\\n```\\n\\n<details>\\n<summary>Exact production proof source</summary>\\n\\n```js\\nimport fs from \\\"node:fs/promises\\\";\\nimport os from \\\"node:os\\\";\\nimport path from \\\"node:path\\\";\\nimport { pathToFileURL } from \\\"node:url\\\";\\n\\nconst repoRoot = process.cwd();\\nconst expectSessionBackground = process.env.OPENCLAW_BUILTIN_PROOF_EXPECT_FIX !== \\\"0\\\";\\nconst proofRoot = await What Problem This Solves\\n\\nZ.AI (GLM) offers a web search API via the MCP `web_search_prime` endpoint, which is **included in the GLM Coding Plan** at no extra cost. However, Assistant currently has no plugin to use it — Z.AI users need a separate Brave API key or another search provider just to get `web_search` functionality.\\n\\nThis plugin lets Z.AI Coding Plan subscribers use `web_search` with zero additional cost or API keys beyond what they already have.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nThe existing `zai` extension only provides LLM inference models and media understanding — no web search. A separate provider (following the brave-plugin pattern) is the right architecture:\\n\\n- **Separate provider ID** (`zai-search`) — doesn't conflict with the existing `zai` model provider\\n- **MCP protocol** — Z.AI exposes search via Model Context Protocol, not a REST API like Brave\\n- **Auto-detect order 20** — lower priority than Brave (10) so Brave stays preferred when both are configured\\n\\n## How It Works\\n\\nThe MCP protocol requires a two-step flow:\\n\\n1. **Initialize** — `POST {baseUrl}` with JSON-RPC `initialize` → captures `Mcp-Session-Id` header\\n2. **Call tool** — `POST {baseUrl}` with `tools/call`, `name: \\\"web_search_prime\\\"`, and the session header → returns SSE-encoded JSON-RPC results\\n\\n### Parameter mapping\\n\\n| openclaw param | Z.AI MCP param |\\n|----------------|-------------------------------|\\n| `query` | `search_query` |\\n| `count` | Slices result array (1–10) |\\n| `country` | `location` (`cn` or `us`) |\\n| `freshness` | `search_recency_filter` |\\n\\n### Double JSON encoding\\n\\nThe MCP `text` content field contains a JSON string which itself wraps a JSON array (doubly escaped). The parser unwraps via iterative `JSON.parse` (up to 2 passes).\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\n- Z.AI Coding Plan users get `web_search` with no extra API key or cost\\n- Falls back automatically if Brave is unavailable (autoDetectOrder: 20)\\n- Same `web_search` tool interface — transparent to agents\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\nTested locally with openclaw 2026.7.1:\\n\\n```\\nweb_search(query=\\\"best pizza recipe 2026\\\", count=2)\\n→ provider: zai-search, count: 2, tookMs: 2451\\n→ results: [{title: \\\"It's Perfect\\\": I Tried King Arthur's 2026 Recipe...\\\", url: thekitchn.com, ...}, ...]\\n```\\n\\n```\\nweb_search(query=\\\"latest technology news August 2026\\\", count=3)\\n→ provider: zai-search, count: 3\\n→ results: [Reuters Tech, GeekWire, TechCrunch, ...]\\n```\\n\\n## Structure\\n\\nFollows the `extensions/brave/` pattern exactly:\\n\\n```\\nextensions/zai-search/\\n├── index.ts # Plugin entry\\n├── openclaw.plugin.json # Plugin manifest\\n├── package.json # npm package\\n├── tsconfig.json # TS config\\n├── README.md # Docs\\n├── web-search-provider.ts # Public barrel\\n├── web-search-shared.ts # Shared metadata\\n├── web-search-contract-api.ts # Contract test API\\n├── test-api.ts # Test helpers barrel\\n└── src/\\n ├── zai-web-search-provider.ts # Provider factory (lazy runtime)\\n └── zai-web-search-provider.runtime.ts # MCP execution\\n```\\n\\n## Notes\\n\\n- The plugin reuses the same Z.AI API key as the existing `zai` model provider (via `ZAI_API_KEY` env var or plugin config)\\n- Credential path: `plugins.entries.zai-search.config.webSearch.apiKey`\\n- No SSRF checks needed — the endpoint is a fixed Z.AI URL (not user-configurable to internal addresses like Brave's self-hosted mode)\", Solves\\n\\nFixes an issue where channel and model selection looked and behaved differently on every surface. Automations rendered channels as a bare native select with no artwork (\\\"last\\\", plus channel names) and models as a free-text field; Agent settings, Model Providers, Session Observer, Talk, and Memory Dreaming each had their own model-selection rendering; chat and new-session embedded a 719-line heavyweight picker. Divergent sentinel handling also caused a real data bug: a retired failure-alert channel silently disappeared from its own dropdown.\\n\\nBefore (Automations channel field):\\n\\n![before: bare native channel select without icons](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/ad7e5847-2296-4471-9ffa-f8fd319dfed5)\\n\\nAfter (chat + Settings, one shared picker with provider artwork):\\n\\n![after: unified chat model picker with icons](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/4c0ce1c0-5673-4d7e-bb38-0e64228b731b)\\n\\n![after: unified settings model picker with icons](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/66925f09-5657-4de5-97e1-685aea99d649)\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nChannel identity presentation and model selection semantics belong to one shared Control UI boundary. This adds `ui/src/components/channel-picker.ts` and `ui/src/components/model-picker.ts` (accessible wa-select idiom, provider/channel artwork, explicit sentinel options, unknown-current values preserved as synthesized entries, optional custom-model entry) and migrates Cron, pairing, wizard, Workboard, Agents, Model Providers, Session Observer, Talk, Memory Dreaming, chat, and new-session onto them. The heavyweight chat picker and its CSS are deleted; catalog loading, mutation locks, and status messaging move to their owning pages; CLI agents move out of the model list into a session-target selector. Compatibility-sensitive semantics are preserved: empty-string default overrides, `null` vs `\\\"\\\"` utility models, alias selectionRefs, slash-bearing ids, Talk's provider-local unqualified ids, chat's async model-switch lock/ordering, and Escape handling.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nEvery place you pick a channel shows the same picker with channel icons; every place you pick a model shows the same compact picker with provider icons. A configured-but-retired channel stays visible in its own field instead of vanishing. The chat/new-session picker is dramatically simpler.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Pre-fix failures: shared-component contract tests failed before the components existed; the Cron regression proved the retired failure-alert channel disappeared pre-fix.\\n- Focused unit runs: 396 tests green (plus 275/63/22/16 in follow-up gate runs); focused browser suites for Cron, Model Providers, aliases, chat switching/ordering, mobile layout, and new-session persistence/reconnect all green.\\n- `check-changed` (lint/type fan-out) green on Testbox.\\n- Source-blind behavior validation: 4/6 scenarios pass (2 blocked by mock-environment limitations); screenshots above are from that run.\\n- Production LOC net −644 (+887/−1531); tests net +116.\\n\", \"created_at\": \"2026-08-13T03:27:03Z\",
